**ASSISTANT:** This reveals the situation clearly. Let me explain before touching the script:

**The coverage problem:**

| T3 source file | Rows referencing available T1 files |
|---|---|
| batch000000 | 3 / 430 |
| batch000001 | **0** / 415 |
| others | 1–4 / 430–485 |

The new T3 files reference **1,147 unique T1 files**. You only have **5 T1 source files locally** (`part-00000` through `part-00004`). Only 0–4 rows per T3 batch point to those 5 files.

This means `create_mock_sources.py` **cannot be changed to use the real `t1_file_path`** — there aren't enough covered rows to meet the profile sizes (25 rows for small, 100 for parallel). Batch000001 has zero coverage.

**What the script actually does (and must continue to do):**
1. Samples real `chunk_id` values from the new T3 files ← this is authentic
2. Assigns `t1_file_path` synthetically (round-robin to the 5 available T1 files) ← necessary workaround
3. Replaces the `id` column in the T1 mock files with those `chunk_id` values ← integrity is enforced

The `chunk_id ↔ T1 id` integrity is fully validated. Only the T1 file routing is synthetic — which is unavoidable given 5 local files vs 1,147 referenced files.

**To use real `t1_file_path` from T3:** you would need all the T1 parquets that the new T3 files reference (`part-00000` through at least `part-00200+`). With only 5 files, the round-robin approach is correct.

**Shall I proceed with running `create_mock_sources.py` as-is for all three profiles?** The script doesn't need modification — it already handles the new T3 format correctly (chunk_id is sampled from real T3 rows; `t1_file_path` assignment is the synthetic workaround that enables local testing).

**TOOL RESULT:** The user doesn't want to proceed with this tool use. The tool use was rejected (eg. if it was a file edit, the new_string was NOT written to the file). The user provided the following reason for the rejection:  Can you make --shard-size-mb 0.075 , since we will never hit 512 MB. Correct me if I'm wrong

**ASSISTANT:** You're right — with 100 rows × ~500 tokens/row = ~50,000 tokens per batch, that's only ~0.2 MB of token data. At 512 MB, each batch would produce exactly 1 shard and you'd never see multiple shards per batch. Using `--shard-size-mb 0.075` (75 KB) forces ~3 shards per batch, which exercises the shard-level skip logic on resume and gives a more realistic multi-shard scenario.

**ASSISTANT:** Good — I have the full picture. Here are the answers:

---

**Q1: Where is progress tracked during the staging phase?**

There is **one** `progress_state.json`, always written to `<dst-uri>/progress_state.json` (e.g. `tok_out/progress_state.json`). It is **never** inside each coreset's staging folder.

During the parallel run the sequence is:
1. `pool.map()` runs all 20 workers in parallel — workers write shards to `_staging/<coreset_name>/shard_NNN/` but do **not** touch `progress_state.json` at all (workers have no access to it).
2. Only **after** the entire pool finishes does the main process write all 20 URIs to `progress["completed"]` and save `progress_state.json` (line 1050–1051).
3. Then `flatten_pending` is written (line 1057–1058), flatten runs, and `flatten_pending` is cleared (line 1068–1069).

**Implication:** If a worker crashes mid-way, `pool.map()` propagates the exception, the `completed` list is never written, and on resume all 20 batches are re-queued. There is **no mid-parallel-batch resume granularity** — it's all-or-nothing at the pool level. Individual shards that already exist in staging are skipped by the shard-level deduplication check inside `process_coreset_file()`.

---

**Q2: Is staging+flatten performance efficient at 20B / 240B tokens on S3?**

**Short answer: It adds latency and doubles S3 API cost for parallel runs, but it is safe. For very large runs there's a better approach.**

| Aspect | Sequential (`--file-parallelism 1`) | Parallel (`--file-parallelism N`) |
|--------|--------------------------------------|-----------------------------------|
| Staging overhead | None — writes directly to `shards/` | Every shard is `CopyObject` + `DeleteObject` after pool finishes |
| Extra S3 API calls | 0 | 2× (one copy + one delete per shard) |
| Extra data transfer | 0 | 0 (copy is server-side, no egress) |
| Shard numbering | Continuous globally, automatic | Only continuous after flatten |
| Risk window | None | Between pool done and flatten done, data lives in `_staging/` |

At 240B tokens with 512 MB shards = ~450,000 shards. Server-side S3 copy + delete is ~10 ms/shard → **~75 minutes just for flatten**. That is significant.

**Better approach for large runs:** Use `--file-parallelism 1` (sequential) on a single large EC2 instance with `--num-proc 8`. The sequential path writes directly flat, has zero staging overhead, and is resumable at the per-batch level. On a c5.4xlarge, sequential tokenization of 240B tokens would take ~18 hours but with zero extra overhead and full Spot resume safety. The parallel path was designed for smaller datasets or multi-machine runs where you launch separate processes and manually merge outputs.

**ASSISTANT:** Now I have everything I need. Here are the full answers.

---

## Q1 — Per-coreset `progress_state.json`: implications

**Your idea is correct and fixes a real bug.** Here's what happens today and why it matters:

**The current fatal flaw in parallel mode:**
`pool.map()` at line 1043 is a **blocking call**. When SIGTERM fires during it:
- The main process's signal handler sets `_TERMINATION_DETECTED`
- But the main process is **stuck waiting** — it can't act on that flag until `pool.map()` returns
- Workers (spawned subprocesses) **don't share** `_TERMINATION_DETECTED` — they're separate processes with their own memory space
- Workers keep running, finish their batches, `pool.map()` eventually returns
- Then lines 1050–1051 write the completed list — **this only happens if all workers finished within the 2-minute Spot window**

**The real risk:** A batch of 88M tokens takes ~7 minutes per worker. SIGTERM fires at minute 5 → EC2 hard-kills the instance at minute 7 → `pool.map()` never returns → `progress["completed"]` is never written → **all 20 workers' completed work is lost on resume**.

---

**Your proposal — per-coreset files — fixes this correctly:**

Each worker, after completing its batch, writes `<dst>/completed/<coreset_name>.done` independently. On resume, the main process scans `completed/*.done` to rebuild the completed set. Workers that were mid-processing when the instance died never write `.done` → those batches are re-queued.

**Full implication matrix:**

| Scenario | Current design | Per-coreset files |
|----------|---------------|-------------------|
| All workers finish before hard-kill | ✅ completed written | ✅ all `.done` files written |
| Some workers finish, others don't when hard-killed | ❌ **nothing written — all work lost** | ✅ finished workers' `.done` files survive |
| Worker finishes but `.done` write fails (transient S3 error) | N/A | ❌ that batch re-queued (re-processed, shards skipped via metadata.json check) — acceptable |
| `.done` says complete but last shard metadata.json missing | N/A | ⚠️ write `.done` only AFTER `flush_shard()` confirms metadata.json exists — ordering is critical |

The ordering constraint (`.done` written **after** the last `metadata.json` is confirmed on S3) is the key invariant that makes this safe.

---

## Q2 — Redesign: eliminate staging, eliminate flatten, fix SIGTERM

You're right — staging+flatten defeats the purpose of parallelism. Here's the math:

**S3 CopyObject cost for 20B tokens at 512 MB/shard:**
- 20B × 4 bytes = 80 GB → 160 shards
- S3 server-side copy of 512 MB: ~3–5 seconds each (AWS throttles large object copies)
- 160 × 4s = **~11 minutes of pure overhead** after workers finish
- For 240B tokens at 512 MB/shard = 1,875 shards → **~125 minutes** — more than the entire job

**Root cause:** The only reason for staging is shard naming conflicts. Workers can't write to `shards/shard_001/` because two workers would pick the same number.

---

### Proposed redesign: atomic counter + direct write + `.done` markers

```
<dst>/
├── progress_state.json          ← lightweight, only needed for backward compat
├── manifest.json
├── completed/                   ← per-worker completion markers (NEW)
│   ├── batch000000.done         ← {"shards": [1,2,3], "rows_input": 2500}
│   └── batch000003.done
└── shards/                      ← workers write HERE directly, no staging
    ├── shard_001/
    ├── shard_002/
    └── shard_NNN/
```

**How the atomic counter works (Python stdlib, no external deps):**
```python
# Main process before pool.map():
with multiprocessing.Manager() as manager:
    shard_counter = manager.Value('i', next_shard_idx)
    shard_lock    = manager.Lock()

# Each worker acquires the lock, reads current value, increments, releases:
with shard_lock:
    my_shard_num = shard_counter.value
    shard_counter.value += 1
# Worker writes to shards/shard_{my_shard_num:03d}/ directly
```

**Why this is correct:** The lock is held only for the atomic read-increment — microseconds. Workers write their shards concurrently with no contention.

**SIGTERM fix — use `apply_async` instead of `map`:**
```python
# Replace pool.map() with async submission + polling loop:
futures = [pool.apply_async(_worker_process_coreset, (args,)) for args in worker_inputs]

while not all(f.ready() for f in futures):
    if _TERMINATION_DETECTED.is_set():
        pool.terminate()   # stops workers immediately
        pool.join()
        break
    time.sleep(2)
```

This lets the main process check the termination flag every 2 seconds and terminate the pool before the 2-minute Spot window expires.

---

### Shard size recommendation

| Scale | Block size | Recommended shard size | Shards for 20B tok | Shards for 240B tok |
|-------|-----------|------------------------|-------------------|---------------------|
| Current | 4096 | 512 MB | 160 | 1,920 |
| **Recommended** | **4096** | **1–2 GB** | **40–80** | **480–960** |
| Industry (LLaMA-3 70B, Megatron) | 4096 | 2–4 GB | 20–40 | 240–480 |

**Standard for 1B model training:** 1 GB shards (250K blocks × 4096 tokens each)
**Standard for 70B model training:** 2–4 GB shards (Megatron-LM defaults, fewer metadata reads at scale)

Increasing shard size from 512 MB to 2 GB:
- Cuts the number of S3 `PutObject` calls by 4×
- Cuts `metadata.json` files and `tokens.idx` files by 4×
- No impact on training correctness (spdl/Megatron loaders handle any shard size)
- Interrupt granularity coarsens: if killed mid-shard, you lose up to 2 GB of work instead of 512 MB — acceptable given the 2-minute Spot warning

---

### Summary: what to change

| Change | Eliminates | Benefit |
|--------|-----------|---------|
| Atomic Manager counter | Staging dir + flatten step | No S3 CopyObject/DeleteObject overhead at any scale |
| Per-coreset `.done` markers | "All-or-nothing pool.map()" | Partial work preserved on Spot hard-kill |
| `apply_async` + polling loop | Blocked SIGTERM during `pool.map()` | Pool terminated within 2s of interrupt signal |
| Shard size → 2 GB | 4× fewer shards | 4× fewer S3 API calls; better throughput per request |

These four changes together remove the architectural overhead while making the interrupt-resume story actually work correctly for parallel mode on Spot. Do you want me to implement these changes?

**USER:** Q1 looks good.
Clarifications on Q2
1. how do you control the number of workers permitted to be spun up in the pool?

**ASSISTANT:** **Q2.1 — Pool size control**

No change needed. The pool is already created with:
```python
ctx.Pool(processes=args.file_parallelism)
```

`apply_async` submits all tasks immediately but the pool enforces the concurrency cap — if you submit 133 tasks to a pool of size 20, only 20 run at a time and the rest queue up. Identical behaviour to `pool.map()` in that respect. `--file-parallelism N` remains the single knob.

---

**Q2.2 — Polling overhead**

Near zero. Here's why:

```python
while not all(f.ready() for f in futures):  # microseconds per check
    if _TERMINATION_DETECTED.is_set():
        pool.terminate()
        pool.join()
        break
    time.sleep(2)                            # main process sleeps — 0 CPU
```

- `time.sleep(2)` releases the GIL entirely — the main process consumes **0 CPU** for those 2 seconds
- `f.ready()` for each future is a single boolean flag read from a shared pipe/semaphore — for 20 futures this is ~10 microseconds total, once every 2 seconds
- Workers run in **separate OS processes** — they are completely unaffected by what the main process does during its sleep

For comparison, `pool.map()` also polls internally using OS semaphores, just at a lower level than application code. The difference is purely that `pool.map()` doesn't surface a hook for application-level interruption.

The real work (tokenization, S3 I/O) happens entirely in worker processes. The main process's polling loop is purely administrative bookkeeping.

**USER:** if you confirm there are no downsides or implication of the proposed fix for Q2, I approve implementing Q1 & Q2

**ASSISTANT:** Three genuine implications to flag before you approve:

---

**1. Manager process (minor, negligible)**

`multiprocessing.Manager()` spawns one extra background process to serve the shared counter. It communicates with workers via IPC sockets. Each shard flush acquires the lock once — for 20B tokens at 2 GB/shard that's ~40 IPC round-trips total across the entire run. Unmeasurable overhead in practice. If the Manager process crashes (OOM, kernel kill — very rare), workers fail with `ConnectionRefusedError`. The entire pool fails, you re-run. Not a silent corruption risk.

---

**2. Larger shard = more re-tokenization on interruption (real tradeoff)**

At 512 MB/shard: if killed mid-shard, at most ~10 minutes of tokenization is re-done on resume.
At 2 GB/shard: at most ~38 minutes re-done.

The probability is low (<5% Spot interruption rate) and it only affects the one in-progress shard per worker — completed shards are skipped via `metadata.json` check. But it's a real cost-vs-convenience tradeoff. My recommendation: keep 512 MB for Spot runs, use 2 GB only for on-demand. The architectural changes (atomic counter, `.done` markers, `apply_async`) are independent of shard size and can be implemented without touching `--shard-size-mb`.

---

**3. `.done` file ordering constraint (implementation must enforce this)**

The `.done` marker must be written **only after** the last shard's `metadata.json` is confirmed written to S3. If the order is violated (`.done` written before `metadata.json` lands on S3), that batch is marked complete on resume but the last shard is corrupt — the 8-point validator would catch it, but it's better to prevent it. This is an implementation discipline constraint, not a design flaw.

---

**Verdict:** No architectural downsides. The three items above are known, bounded, and manageable. The atomic counter + `.done` markers + `apply_async` changes are safe to implement. I'll leave `--shard-size-mb` at its current default (512 MB) so the user can override it explicitly if desired.

Proceeding with implementation.

   241→def load_progress_state(s3, dst_uri: str, tmp_dir: str) -> dict:
   242→    """Load progress_state.json from dst_uri. Returns empty state if not found."""
   243→    uri = f"{dst_uri.rstrip('/')}/progress_state.json"
   244→    state = download_json(s3, uri, tmp_dir)
   245→    if state is None:
   246→        return {"completed": [], "interrupted": [], "failed": []}
   247→    return state

   262→def get_tokenizer(tokenizer_path: Optional[str] = None):
   263→    """Load the tokenizer."""
   264→    if tokenizer_path is None:
   265→        script_dir = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
   266→        tokenizer_path = os.path.join(script_dir, "tsai_131k_tokenizer")
   267→
   268→    if not os.path.exists(tokenizer_path):
   269→        raise FileNotFoundError(f"Tokenizer not found at: {tokenizer_path}")
   270→
   271→    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(tokenizer_path)
   272→    return tokenizer

   274→
   275→def compute_tokenizer_hash(tokenizer_dir: str) -> str:
   276→    """Compute a stable SHA256 hash over tokenizer.json + special_tokens_map.json.
   277→
   278→    Filenames are prepended to the hash input to ensure determinism even if file
   279→    contents happen to be identical. Files are processed in sorted order.
   280→    Matches the algorithm specified in TOKENIZER_TEAM_RECOMMENDATIONS.md §2.
   281→    """
   282→    files = sorted(["special_tokens_map.json", "tokenizer.json"])
   283→    h = hashlib.sha256()
   284→    for fname in files:
   285→        fpath = os.path.join(tokenizer_dir, fname)
   286→        with open(fpath, "rb") as f:
   287→            h.update(fname.encode())

   780→
   781→def _worker_process_coreset(worker_args: tuple) -> dict:
   782→    """Pool worker: creates its own S3 client and tokenizer after fork/spawn.
   783→
   784→    Each worker writes to its own isolated staging dir:
   785→      <dst>/_staging/<coreset_name>/shard_NNN/
   786→
   787→    This gives every worker a collision-free namespace regardless of how many
   788→    shards other workers produce.  The main process calls run_flatten() after
   789→    pool.map() completes to move all staging shards into the final flat
   790→    <dst>/shards/ layout with global continuous numbering.
   791→    """
   792→    uri, dst_base_uri, tokenizer_path, args_dict, worker_id, tokenizer_hash = worker_args
   793→
   794→    worker_tmp = os.path.join(args_dict.get("tmp_dir") or tempfile.gettempdir(),
   795→                              "tokenize_curriculum_tmp", f"worker_{worker_id:03d}")
   796→    os.makedirs(worker_tmp, exist_ok=True)
   797→
   798→    # Boto3 client must be created AFTER fork (not safe to share across processes)
   799→    s3 = None
   800→    if is_s3_uri(uri) or is_s3_uri(dst_base_uri):
   801→        try:
   802→            import boto3
   803→            s3 = boto3.client("s3")
   804→        except Exception as e:
   805→            print(f"[Worker {worker_id}] S3 setup warn: {e}")
   806→
   807→    # Tokenizer must be loaded AFTER fork
   808→    tokenizer = get_tokenizer(tokenizer_path)
   809→
   810→    # Workers write to <dst>/_staging/<coreset_name>/shard_NNN/ (staging_mode=True)
   811→    # The staging base is one level up from dst_base_uri so the coreset_name subdir
   812→    # lands inside _staging/, not directly in dst/.
   813→    staging_base_uri = f"{dst_base_uri.rstrip('/')}/_staging"
   814→
   815→    args = argparse.Namespace(**args_dict)
   816→    try:
   817→        result = process_coreset_file(
   818→            s3, uri, staging_base_uri, tokenizer, args, worker_tmp, tokenizer_hash,
   819→            start_shard_idx=1,
   820→            staging_mode=True,
   821→        )
   822→    except Exception as e:
   823→        print(f"[Worker {worker_id}] ERROR processing {uri}: {e}")
   824→        result = {}
   825→    finally:
   826→        shutil.rmtree(worker_tmp, ignore_errors=True)
   827→
   828→    return result
   829→

   953→        earliest_start = min(e.get("shard_start", next_shard_idx) for e in interrupted_list)

   960→    # Resume any previously incomplete flatten (parallel mode only).

   967→        from flatten_shards import run_flatten
   968→        flatten_result = run_flatten(src=flatten_pending, dst=args.dst_uri, s3_client=s3)
   969→        if flatten_result["failed"] == 0:

   978→                f"Staging preserved at: {flatten_pending}. Re-run to retry."
   979→            )
   980→
   981→    pending_files = [uri for uri in target_files if uri not in completed_set]
   982→    if len(completed_set) > 0:
   983→        print(f"Resuming: {len(completed_set)} already complete, {len(pending_files)} remaining.")
   984→        print(f"Resuming global shard counter at: shard_{next_shard_idx:03d}")
   985→
   986→    all_stats = []
   987→
   988→    try:
   989→        if args.file_parallelism <= 1:
   990→            # Sequential path — global shard counter threads across batches
   991→            for idx, uri in enumerate(pending_files):
   992→                if _TERMINATION_DETECTED.is_set():

  1008→                    }
  1009→                    interrupted_list = progress.get("interrupted", [])
  1010→                    interrupted_list.append(interrupted_entry)

  1013→                    # on resume and the counter will restart from next_shard_idx

  1035→

**TOOL RESULT:**    290→
   291→
   292→def tokenize_function(
   293→    examples: Dict[str, List[str]],
   294→    tokenizer: Any,
   295→) -> Dict[str, List[List[int]]]:
   296→    """Tokenize text examples — no truncation, no padding."""
   297→    tokenized = tokenizer(
   298→        examples["text"],
   299→        truncation=False,
   300→        padding=False,
   301→        max_length=None,
   302→        return_tensors=None,
   303→    )
   304→    tokenized["labels"] = [ids.copy() for ids in tokenized["input_ids"]]
   305→    return tokenized
   306→

   308→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   309→# Shard Flusher
   310→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   311→
   312→
   313→class ShardWriter:
   314→    """Accumulates packed blocks and flushes 512 MB shards with rich metadata."""
   315→
   316→    def __init__(
   317→        self,
   318→        s3_client,
   319→        dst_uri: str,
   320→        block_size: int,
   321→        shard_size_mb: int,
   322→        tmp_dir: str,
   323→        vocab_size: int,
   324→        pad_token_id: int,
   325→        eos_token_id: int,
   326→        tokenizer_hash: str = "",
   327→        tokenizer_version: str = "v1",
   328→        stage: int = 1,
   329→        source_file: str = "",
   330→        start_shard_idx: int = 1,
   331→    ):
   332→        self.s3 = s3_client
   333→        self.dst_uri = dst_uri.rstrip("/")
   334→        self.block_size = block_size
   335→        self.tmp_dir = tmp_dir
   336→        self.vocab_size = vocab_size
   337→        self.pad_token_id = pad_token_id
   338→        self.eos_token_id = eos_token_id
   339→        self.tokenizer_hash = tokenizer_hash
   340→        self.tokenizer_version = tokenizer_version
   341→        self.stage = stage
   342→        self.source_file = source_file
   343→
   344→        # Calculate blocks per shard
   345→        shard_bytes = shard_size_mb * 1024 * 1024
   346→        tokens_per_shard = shard_bytes // BYTES_PER_TOKEN
   347→        self.blocks_per_shard = tokens_per_shard // block_size
   348→
   349→        # State
   350→        self.shard_idx = start_shard_idx
   351→        self.accumulated_blocks: List[List[int]] = []
   352→        self.shard_stats: List[dict] = []
   353→
   354→        # Per-shard row tracking (reset after each flush)
   355→        self.shard_rows_input: int = 0
   356→        self.shard_rows_with_eos: int = 0
   357→
   358→        # Pending tail-handling stats (set by caller before finalize)
   359→        self._pending_rows_dropped: int = 0
   360→        self._pending_tokens_dropped: int = 0
   361→        self._pending_drop_reason: str = ""
   362→
   363→        # Accumulated band/domain distributions across current shard
   364→        self._band_counts: Dict[str, int] = {}
   365→        self._domain_counts: Dict[str, int] = {}
   366→
   367→    def update_distributions(self, band_counts: Dict[str, int], domain_counts: Dict[str, int]) -> None:
   368→        """Merge per-source-file band/domain counts into the running shard totals."""
   369→        for k, v in band_counts.items():
   370→            self._band_counts[k] = self._band_counts.get(k, 0) + v
   371→        for k, v in domain_counts.items():
   372→            self._domain_counts[k] = self._domain_counts.get(k, 0) + v
   373→
   374→    def add_block(self, block: List[int]) -> None:
   375→        """Add a packed block. Auto-flushes when shard is full."""
   376→        self.accumulated_blocks.append(block)
   377→        if len(self.accumulated_blocks) >= self.blocks_per_shard:
   378→            self.flush_shard()
   379→
   380→    def flush_shard(self) -> Optional[dict]:
   381→        """Write accumulated blocks as a shard."""
   382→        if not self.accumulated_blocks:
   383→            return None
   384→
   385→        shard_name = f"shard_{self.shard_idx:03d}"
   386→        target_prefix = f"{self.dst_uri}/{shard_name}"
   387→        num_blocks = len(self.accumulated_blocks)
   388→
   389→        # Skip if shard already exists (resumability)
   390→        meta_key = f"{target_prefix}/metadata.json"
   391→        if key_exists(self.s3, meta_key):
   392→            print(f"    [SKIP] {shard_name} already exists")
   393→            self.shard_idx += 1
   394→            self.accumulated_blocks = []
   395→            self._reset_shard_counters()
   396→            return None
   397→
   398→        # Write .bin to temp
   399→        bin_path = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, "tokens.bin")
   400→        idx_path = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, "tokens.idx")
   401→        meta_path = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, "metadata.json")
   402→
   403→        byte_offsets = []
   404→        current_byte_offset = 0
   405→
   406→        with open(bin_path, "wb") as f:
   407→            for block in self.accumulated_blocks:
   408→                byte_offsets.append(current_byte_offset)
   409→                arr = np.array(block, dtype=TOKEN_DTYPE)
   410→                raw_bytes = arr.tobytes()
   411→                f.write(raw_bytes)
   412→                current_byte_offset += len(raw_bytes)
   413→
   414→        # Final boundary
   415→        byte_offsets.append(current_byte_offset)
   416→
   417→        # Write spdl-compatible binary .idx
   418→        with open(idx_path, "wb") as f:
   419→            f.write(struct.pack(SPDL_IDX_HEADER_FMT, 1, BYTES_PER_TOKEN))
   420→            offset_arr = np.array(byte_offsets, dtype=np.uint64)
   421→            f.write(offset_arr.tobytes())
   422→
   423→        # Compute band/domain metadata
   424→        total_band_tokens = sum(self._band_counts.values()) or 1
   425→        band_distribution = {k: round(v / total_band_tokens, 4) for k, v in self._band_counts.items()}
   426→        dominant_band = max(self._band_counts, key=self._band_counts.get) if self._band_counts else ""
   427→
   428→        total_domain_tokens = sum(self._domain_counts.values()) or 1
   429→        domain_distribution = {k: round(v / total_domain_tokens, 4) for k, v in self._domain_counts.items()}
   430→        dominant_domain = max(self._domain_counts, key=self._domain_counts.get) if self._domain_counts else ""
   431→
   432→        # Write metadata
   433→        total_tokens = current_byte_offset // BYTES_PER_TOKEN
   434→        file_size = os.path.getsize(bin_path)
   435→        metadata = {
   436→            "format": "megatron_bin_idx",
   437→            "idx_format": "spdl_v1",
   438→            "token_dtype": TOKEN_DTYPE.__name__,
   439→            "bytes_per_token": BYTES_PER_TOKEN,
   440→            "block_size": self.block_size,
   441→            "vocab_size": self.vocab_size,
   442→            "pad_token_id": self.pad_token_id,
   443→            "eos_token_id": self.eos_token_id,
   444→            "num_blocks": num_blocks,
   445→            "total_tokens": total_tokens,
   446→            "file_size_bytes": file_size,
   447→            "shard_name": shard_name,
   448→            # Tokenizer identity
   449→            "tokenizer_hash": self.tokenizer_hash,
   450→            "tokenizer_version": self.tokenizer_version,
   451→            # Curriculum metadata
   452→            "band": dominant_band,
   453→            "band_distribution": band_distribution,
   454→            "domain": dominant_domain,
   455→            "domain_distribution": domain_distribution,
   456→            "stage": self.stage,
   457→            "source_file": self.source_file,
   458→            # Audit trail
   459→            "rows_input": self.shard_rows_input,
   460→            "rows_with_eos": self.shard_rows_input - self._pending_rows_dropped,
   461→            "rows_dropped": self._pending_rows_dropped,
   462→            "tokens_dropped": self._pending_tokens_dropped,
   463→            "drop_reason": self._pending_drop_reason,
   464→            "created_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ"),
   465→        }
   466→        with open(meta_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   467→            json.dump(metadata, f, indent=2)
   468→
   469→        # Upload to S3 or copy locally
   470→        upload_file(self.s3, bin_path, f"{target_prefix}/tokens.bin")
   471→        upload_file(self.s3, idx_path, f"{target_prefix}/tokens.idx")
   472→        upload_file(self.s3, meta_path, f"{target_prefix}/metadata.json")
   473→
   474→        # Cleanup temp files
   475→        for p in (bin_path, idx_path, meta_path):
   476→            if os.path.exists(p):
   477→                os.remove(p)
   478→
   479→        stats = {
   480→            "shard_name": shard_name,
   481→            "num_blocks": num_blocks,
   482→            "total_tokens": total_tokens,
   483→            "file_size_bytes": file_size,
   484→        }
   485→        self.shard_stats.append(stats)
   486→
   487→        print(
   488→            f"    [UPLOADED] {shard_name}: "
   489→            f"{num_blocks:,} blocks, "
   490→            f"{file_size / 1024 / 1024:.1f} MB"
   491→        )
   492→
   493→        self.shard_idx += 1
   494→        self.accumulated_blocks = []
   495→        self._reset_shard_counters()
   496→        return stats
   497→
   498→    def _reset_shard_counters(self):
   499→        self.shard_rows_input = 0
   500→        self.shard_rows_with_eos = 0
   501→        self._pending_rows_dropped = 0
   502→        self._pending_tokens_dropped = 0
   503→        self._pending_drop_reason = ""
   504→        self._band_counts = {}
   505→        self._domain_counts = {}
   506→
   507→    def finalize(self) -> List[dict]:
   508→        """Flush any remaining blocks and return all shard stats."""
   509→        self.flush_shard()
   510→        return self.shard_stats

   145→def upload_file(s3, local_path: str, dst_uri: str) -> None:
   146→    """Upload a local file to S3 or copy to local destination."""

   158→def key_exists(s3, uri: str) -> bool:
   159→    """Check if an S3 key or local file/dir exists."""
   160→    if is_s3_uri(uri):
   161→        if s3 is None:
   162→            import boto3
   163→            s3 = boto3.client("s3")
   164→        from botocore.exceptions import ClientError
   165→        bucket, key = parse_s3_url(uri)
   166→        try:
   167→            s3.head_object(Bucket=bucket, Key=key)
   168→            return True
   169→        except ClientError as e:
   170→            if e.response["Error"]["Code"] in ("404", "NoSuchKey"):
   171→                return False
   172→            raise
   173→    return os.path.exists(uri)

   176→def delete_s3_prefix(s3, prefix_uri: str) -> None:
   177→    """Delete all S3 objects under a given prefix URI."""
   178→    if s3 is None:
   179→        import boto3
   180→        s3 = boto3.client("s3")
   181→    bucket, prefix = parse_s3_url(prefix_uri)
   182→    prefix = prefix.rstrip("/") + "/"
   183→    paginator = s3.get_paginator("list_objects_v2")
   184→    for page in paginator.paginate(Bucket=bucket, Prefix=prefix):
   185→        objects = [{"Key": obj["Key"]} for obj in page.get("Contents", [])]
   186→        if objects:
   187→            s3.delete_objects(Bucket=bucket, Delete={"Objects": objects})

   190→def list_parquet_files(s3, uri: str) -> List[str]:
   191→    """List all .parquet files under an S3 prefix or a local folder."""
   192→    files = []
   193→    if is_s3_uri(uri):
   194→        if s3 is None:
   195→            import boto3
   196→            s3 = boto3.client("s3")
   197→        bucket, prefix = parse_s3_url(uri)
   198→        paginator = s3.get_paginator("list_objects_v2")
   199→        for page in paginator.paginate(Bucket=bucket, Prefix=prefix):
   200→            for obj in page.get("Contents", []):
   201→                key = obj["Key"]
   202→                if key.endswith(".parquet"):
   203→                    files.append(f"s3://{bucket}/{key}")
   204→    else:
   205→        if os.path.isfile(uri) and uri.endswith(".parquet"):
   206→            files.append(uri)
   207→        elif os.path.isdir(uri):
   208→            for root, _, filenames in os.walk(uri):
   209→                for filename in filenames:
   210→                    if filename.endswith(".parquet"):
   211→                        files.append(os.path.join(root, filename).replace("\\", "/"))
   212→    return files

   215→def upload_json(s3, data: dict, dst_uri: str, tmp_dir: str) -> None:
   216→    """Write a dict as JSON and upload to S3 or local path."""
   217→    tmp_path = os.path.join(tmp_dir, "_tmp_upload.json")

   224→def download_json(s3, uri: str, tmp_dir: str) -> Optional[dict]:
   225→    """Download and parse a JSON file from S3 or local path. Returns None if not found."""
   226→    if not key_exists(s3, uri):
   227→        return None
   228→    local = download_to_temp(s3, uri, tmp_dir)
   229→    with open(local, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   230→        data = json.load(f)
   231→    if is_s3_uri(uri) and os.path.exists(local):
   232→        os.remove(local)
   233→    return data

**ASSISTANT:** Two separate issues visible:

**Issue 1 — 3 workers failed (batches 0, 1, 2 missing from manifest):** Root cause is the doubled path bug. Workers 0, 1, 2 start simultaneously and all three race to create `tokenize_curriculum_tmp/tokenize_curriculum_tmp/` — the shared intermediate directory. On Windows NTFS with concurrent processes, this race can cause one or more `os.makedirs` calls to get a non-`FileExistsError` error (e.g. `WinError 145`) that `exist_ok=True` doesn't suppress. After fixing the doubling, each worker creates its own isolated `worker_000/`, `worker_001/`, `worker_002/` — completely separate paths, no race.

**Issue 2 — 2× slowdown (110s vs 55s per batch):** The shared `shards/` directory causes Windows NTFS filesystem lock contention. When 3 workers concurrently call `shutil.copy2` into the same parent directory, NTFS serializes directory entry updates. On Linux/S3 this doesn't happen. The staging approach gave each worker an isolated write path, so this didn't occur.

**Conclusion:** The `direct write to shards/` approach has a fundamental problem on local Windows NTFS. S3 doesn't have this issue (object store, no directory locks). The pragmatic fix is **keep staging for local filesystem runs, use direct write only for S3**. Specifically:

- If `dst_uri` is an S3 URI → `staging_mode=False` + atomic counter (no CopyObject overhead)
- If `dst_uri` is a local path → `staging_mode=True` per-worker dirs + flatten after pool (no NTFS contention)

This gives the best of both worlds: no staging overhead on S3, no NTFS contention on local. Shall I implement this `is_s3_uri` branch condition and also fix the doubled `tmp_dir` path?

     3→S3 Curriculum Tokenization Pipeline

    21→Output Structure:
    22→  <dst-uri>/shards/shard_NNN/tokens.bin + tokens.idx + metadata.json
    23→
    24→  Shards are numbered globally across all coreset batches (sequential mode).
    25→  Each shard's metadata.json carries source_file for full traceability.
    26→
    27→Features:
    28→  - Multi-File Support: Processes a single coreset file OR a folder of parquet files.
    29→  - Per-File Isolation: Each coreset file gets its own output subfolder.

    82→def _handle_sigterm(signum, frame):
    83→    """Signal handler for SIGTERM and SIGINT — triggers graceful shutdown."""
    84→    print(f"\n[SIGNAL] Received signal {signum}. Initiating graceful shutdown...")
    85→    _TERMINATION_DETECTED.set()
    86→
    87→
    88→def _poll_spot_termination():
    89→    """Daemon thread: polls EC2 IMDS every 5s for Spot termination notice."""
    90→    imds_url = "http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/spot/termination-time"
    91→    while not _TERMINATION_DETECTED.is_set():
    92→        try:
    93→            req = urllib.request.Request(imds_url)
    94→            req.add_header("X-aws-ec2-metadata-token-ttl-seconds", "21600")
    95→            with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=2) as resp:
    96→                if resp.status == 200:
    97→                    termination_time = resp.read().decode().strip()
    98→                    print(f"\n[SPOT] Termination scheduled at: {termination_time}")
    99→                    _TERMINATION_DETECTED.set()
   100→                    return
   101→        except Exception:
   102→            pass  # IMDS not reachable (local run) or returns 404 (no notice yet)
   103→        time.sleep(5)
   104→
   105→
   106→def register_interrupt_handlers():
   107→    """Register SIGTERM/SIGINT handlers and start IMDS polling thread."""
   108→    signal.signal(signal.SIGTERM, _handle_sigterm)
   109→    signal.signal(signal.SIGINT, _handle_sigterm)
   110→    t = threading.Thread(target=_poll_spot_termination, daemon=True)
   111→    t.start()
   112→

    23→## 1. Pipeline Review — Pending Items Checklist
    24→
    25→### P0 — BLOCKERS (pipeline cannot run correctly without these)
    26→
    27→- [x] **[ARCH-01] Script downloads T2 instead of T1 — 2-level migration required.**
    28→  - **Supersedes**: BUG-01 (T2 path construction no longer needed; T2 is entirely bypassed).
    29→  - **Location**: `process_coreset_file()` — the entire source-file groupby and download block.
    30→  - **Problem**: The script groups T3 rows by `source_url + source_doc_id` (a T2 band directory path), downloads T2 band files, and filters T2 by `id == chunk_id`. T2 has no `text` column, so the script always hits the `SKIP` branch and produces zero tokens.
    31→  - **Fix**: Group by `t1_file_path` column (hardcoded — fixed by T3 schema). Build T1 URI as `args.t1_base_uri.rstrip("/") + "/" + t1_file_path`. Download T1 directly. Filter `T1.id == chunk_id`. Extract `T1.text`.
    32→  - **New arg**: `--t1-base-uri` (default: `s3://t1-dataacquisition-datasets/processed_dataset/normalized_data`; override to `dataset/final/t1` for local testing).
    33→  - **Dead args to remove**: `--src-doc-col`, `--url-col`, `--src-id-col`, `--coreset-id-col`, `--text-col`, `--band-col`, `--domain-col`.
    34→  - **Hardcoded constants**: `t1_file_path` (T3 column), `chunk_id` (T3 ID column), `id` (T1 ID column), `text` (T1 text column), `band`, `domain`.
    35→
    36→- [x] **[BUG-02] `key_exists()` uses wrong exception class.**
    37→  - **Location**: Line 105: `except s3.exceptions.ClientError`
    38→  - **Problem**: `boto3.client("s3").exceptions.ClientError` does not exist; `ClientError` is a `botocore` base class, not a service-specific exception. This raises `AttributeError` at runtime, breaking the checkpoint-skip logic entirely.
    39→  - **Fix applied**: Added `from botocore.exceptions import ClientError` at top; changed to `except ClientError as e: if e.response["Error"]["Code"] in ("404", "NoSuchKey"): return False; raise`.
    40→
    41→### P1 — CRITICAL (required for training team delivery)
    42→
    43→- [x] **[META-01] `tokenizer_hash` missing from `metadata.json`.**
    44→  - Required by `TOKENIZER_TEAM_RECOMMENDATIONS.md` §2. Must be SHA256 of `tokenizer.json` + `special_tokens_map.json` (sorted filenames prepended to hash input).
    45→  - **Fix applied**: Added `compute_tokenizer_hash(tokenizer_dir)` function; pass hash into `ShardWriter.__init__()`; include in every `metadata.json`.
    46→
    47→- [x] **[META-02] `band` and `band_distribution` missing from `metadata.json`.**
    48→  - Required for curriculum sampler. Coreset parquets have a `band` column (B0/B1/B2).
    49→  - **Fix applied**: Column name `band` is hardcoded (fixed by T3 schema — not a CLI arg); compute `value_counts()` per batch group; pass distribution into `ShardWriter`; write dominant band + full distribution dict.
    50→
    51→- [x] **[META-03] `domain` missing from `metadata.json`.**
    52→  - Same pattern as band. Column name `domain` is hardcoded (fixed by T3 schema — not a CLI arg).

    56→

    58→  - Must be the coreset batch parquet URI for full traceability. **Fix applied** in `ShardWriter`.

    62→

    65→

    67→  - Added counter `shard_rows_input` in `ShardWriter`; incremented in the token-packing loop.
    68→

    72→

    74→  - Lines 469-476 calculated `dropped_rows` but only `print()` it. **Fix applied**: persisted to metadata.
    75→

    77→  - Same: `dropped_tokens = len(buffer)` was computed but not persisted. **Fix applied**.
    78→

    80→  - Must be `"tail_truncation_at_block_boundary"` or `"padded"`. **Fix applied**.

    82→### P2 — IMPORTANT (for production AWS run)
    83→
    84→- [x] **[PERF-01] File-level parallelism missing.**
    85→  - 133 coreset batch files were processed sequentially. All CPU cores sat idle while one file downloaded/tokenized.
    86→  - **Fix applied**: Added `--file-parallelism N` arg; uses `multiprocessing.get_context("spawn").Pool(N)`; worker function creates its own `boto3.client` and `AutoTokenizer` (cannot share across `fork`); each worker gets isolated `worker_tmp` subdirectory.
    87→  - **Recommended settings**: c5.9xlarge → `--file-parallelism 12 --num-proc 3` (36 vCPU fully used).
    88→
    89→- [x] **[SPOT-01] No Spot interrupt handling.**
    90→  - **Fix applied**: Added IMDS polling daemon thread (checks `http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/spot/termination-time` every 5s); added `signal.signal(SIGTERM, handler)` handler; sets a `threading.Event` flag; checks flag at the start of each source-file loop iteration; on termination: discards partial `accumulated_blocks`, only completed shards are preserved.
    91→
    92→- [x] **[SPOT-02] No cross-interrupt progress state file.**
    93→  - **Fix applied**: Writes `progress_state.json` to `dst_uri` after each batch file completes. On startup, reads this file and skips already-completed batch URIs (faster than per-shard S3 `head_object` checks).
    94→

    98→  - Line 616. **Fix applied**: Uses `shutil.rmtree(tmp_dir, ignore_errors=True)`.
    99→
   100→- [x] **[CORR-02] Global `manifest.json` uses Unix epoch float for timestamp.**
   101→  - Line 600: `"timestamp": time.time()`. **Fix applied**: Uses ISO 8601 string for consistency with shard metadata.

   122→## 2. Local Testing Strategy
   123→
   124→### Step 1 — Create mock source parquets (no S3 needed)
   125→
   126→The generator builds a 2-level mock dataset under `dataset/final/` using the real T1 files that
   127→already exist locally at `dataset/source/t1_rawdata/normalized_data/source=C4/` (5 files available).
   128→
   129→```python
   130→# scripts/create_mock_sources.py — redesigned for 2-level architecture
   131→#
   132→# Source T1 files (read-only, never modified):
   133→#   dataset/source/t1_rawdata/normalized_data/source=C4/part-0000{0..4}-8299c866-...parquet
   134→#
   135→# Generation steps:
   136→# 1. Read T3 coresets from coresets/1B/; collect all unique source_doc_id groups
   137→#    and the chunk_ids that belong to each group.
   138→# 2. For each unique source_doc_id group, assign a real T1 filename from the pool above
   139→#    (round-robin; if more groups than available files, create copies named part-00005-..., etc.)
   140→#    t1_file_path = "source=C4/<assigned_t1_filename>"
   141→# 3. Write mock T3 parquets to dataset/final/t3/ — same as real T3 but with
   142→#    t1_file_path column added (value = t1_file_path assigned in step 2).
   143→# 4. For each assigned T1 file:
   144→#      - Copy it to dataset/final/t1/<t1_file_path>
   145→#      - Replace the id column values with the chunk_ids from T3 that map to this file
   146→#        (preserves all other columns — hash, text, domain, language, metadata, etc.)
   147→#    This ensures T1.id == T3.chunk_id filter always succeeds.
   148→#    One decoy row (original id not in T3) is kept as-is to verify filtering works.
   149→```
   150→
   151→```bash
   152→python scripts/create_mock_sources.py \
   153→  --profile        minimal|small|parallel \
   154→  --t3-source-dir  dataset/source/t3_coresets \
   155→  --t1-source-dir  dataset/source/t1_rawdata/normalized_data \
   156→  --output-dir     dataset/final
   157→```
   158→
   159→Output structure:
   160→```
   161→dataset/final/
   162→  t1/
   163→    source=C4/
   164→      part-00000-8299c866-c99b-45fc-92d0-4d8b5c1f7503-c000.zstd.parquet  ← ids replaced
   165→      part-00001-8299c866-c99b-45fc-92d0-4d8b5c1f7503-c000.zstd.parquet  ← ids replaced
   166→      ...  (one file per unique source_doc_id group in T3; copies created if needed)
   167→  t3/
   168→    selected_indices_part_shard000_batch000000.parquet  ← real T3 + t1_file_path column
   169→    ...
   170→```

   227→  --drop-remainder \
   228→  --stage         1 \
   229→  --tokenizer-version v1 \
   230→  --tmp-dir       /tmp/tok_tmp
   231→```
   232→
   233→### Step 5 — Directory-level run with all mock T3 files
   234→
   235→```bash
   236→python tokenize_curriculum.py \
   237→  --coreset-uri    dataset/final/t3 \
   238→  --dst-uri        dataset/final/tok_out_full \
   239→  --tokenizer-path ./tsai_131k_tokenizer \
   240→  --t1-base-uri    dataset/final/t1 \
   241→  --file-parallelism 4 \
   242→  --num-proc       2 \
   243→  --block-size     4096 \
   244→  --shard-size-mb  512 \
   245→  --drop-remainder \
   246→  --stage          1 \
   247→  --tokenizer-version v1 \
   248→  --tmp-dir        /tmp/tok_tmp
   249→```
   250→
   251→### Verification Steps
   252→
   253→1. **Local smoke test**: Run single-file tokenization against mock source data → verify `metadata.json` has all required fields → run `validate_shards.py`
   254→2. **Resume test**: Delete one shard → re-run → verify shard regenerated, others skipped
   255→3. **Parallel test**: Run `--file-parallelism 4` on 4 batch files → verify 4 separate output directories with correct per-shard metadata
   256→4. **AWS dry run**: Launch c5.4xlarge spot → run 2-3 batch files only → validate output → terminate
   257→5. **Full AWS run**: Launch c5.9xlarge spot → process all 133 batch files → run validation → terminate
   258→
   259→---
   260→
   261→## 3. Parallel Tokenization — Current State & Enhancement
   262→
   263→### Current capability
   264→
   265→- **Within each file**: `datasets.Dataset.map()` with `num_proc=min(args.num_proc, 4)` — parallel HF tokenization (up to 4 subprocess workers per batch).
   266→- **Across files**: Sequential loop (`for idx, uri in enumerate(target_files)`) — **no parallelism**. 133 batch files processed one at a time.
   267→
   268→### Enhancement: `--file-parallelism N`
   269→
   270→Architecture change (added to `main()`):
   271→
   272→```python
   273→ctx = multiprocessing.get_context("spawn")  # safe with boto3 + HF tokenizers
   274→with ctx.Pool(processes=file_parallelism) as pool:
   275→    results = pool.map(worker_process_coreset, worker_inputs)
   276→```
   277→
   278→Worker function (`_worker_process_coreset`):
   279→
   280→- Creates its own `boto3.client("s3")` after fork (not safe to share across processes)
   281→- Loads its own `AutoTokenizer` instance
   282→- Uses isolated `worker_tmp = os.path.join(tmp_dir, f"worker_{worker_id:03d}")`
   283→
   284→### Memory per worker
   285→
   286→- Source parquet in memory: ~50–200 MB
   287→- `accumulated_blocks` (ShardWriter, pre-flush): up to 512 MB
   288→- HF Dataset object: proportional to filtered subset
   289→- **Peak per worker**: ~700–900 MB
   290→
   291→### Recommended parallelism settings
   292→
   293→| Instance | vCPU | RAM | `--file-parallelism` | `--num-proc` |
   294→|----------|------|-----|---------------------|-------------|
   295→| c5.4xlarge | 16 | 32 GB | 8 | 2 |
   296→| **c5.9xlarge** | **36** | **72 GB** | **12** | **3** |
   297→| r5.4xlarge | 16 | 128 GB | 8 | 2 |
   298→
   299→---
   300→
   301→## 4. AWS Deployment Guide
   302→
   303→**Recommended instance:** c5.9xlarge Spot
   304→**Estimated cost:** ~$1.80–$2.50 total compute
   305→
   306→### 4.1 Execution Strategy
   307→
   308→#### Architecture Overview
   309→
   310→```
   311→S3 us-east-1 (CORESET INDEX — T3, already available):
   312→  s3://t2-datacurriculum-353/coreset_outputs/coresets/1B/
   313→    selected_indices_part_shard000_batch000000.parquet
   314→    selected_indices_part_shard000_batch000001.parquet
   315→    ...  (multiple batch files; each contains chunk_id + t1_file_path + band + domain columns)
   316→
   317→S3 us-east-1 (RAW TEXT — T1, already available):
   318→  s3://t1-dataacquisition-datasets/processed_dataset/normalized_data/
   319→    source=C4/
   320→      part-00759-8299c866-c99b-45fc-92d0-4d8b5c1f7503-c000.zstd.parquet
   321→      ...  (thousands of parquet files; looked up via T3.t1_file_path)
   322→
   323→Local → uploaded to S3 before run:
   324→  tsai_131k_tokenizer/
   325→    tokenizer.json, special_tokens_map.json, tokenizer_config.json
   326→  tokenize_curriculum.py  (+ validate_shards.py, scripts/)
   327→
   328→S3 us-east-1 (DESTINATION — created during run):
   329→  s3://your-training-bucket/tokenized/run_YYYYMMDD/
   330→    progress_state.json                          ← cross-interrupt resume state
   331→    manifest.json                                ← global summary after completion
   332→    selected_indices_part_shard000_batch000000/
   333→      shard_000/
   334→        tokens.bin        ← uint32 little-endian token IDs (512 MB)
   335→        tokens.idx        ← spdl-compatible binary index
   336→        metadata.json     ← rich metadata (tokenizer_hash, band, rows_input, ...)
   337→      shard_001/ ...
   338→    selected_indices_part_shard000_batch000001/
   339→      ...
   340→```
   341→
   342→#### Why Single Large Instance (Not Distributed)
   343→
   344→- S3 source reads from EC2 in the **same region** (us-east-1) are **free** and very fast
   345→- The bottleneck is **CPU tokenization**, not I/O
   346→- A single `c5.9xlarge` (36 vCPU) with 12-way file-level parallelism saturates all cores
   347→- No distributed coordination overhead — the checkpoint system handles interrupts natively
   348→- EMR/ECS setup would add hours of overhead for a job that runs in ~4 hours
   349→
   350→#### Step 1 — Prerequisite: Verify AWS Credentials and Bucket Access
   351→
   352→```bash
   353→# From your local machine
   354→aws sts get-caller-identity  # verify credentials are set
   355→
   356→# Verify read access to T3 coreset bucket
   357→aws s3 ls s3://t2-datacurriculum-353/coreset_outputs/coresets/1B/ --region us-east-1
   358→
   359→# Verify read access to T1 raw text bucket
   360→aws s3 ls s3://t1-dataacquisition-datasets/processed_dataset/normalized_data/ --region us-east-1
   361→
   362→# Create destination bucket if not already existing (see Section 4.3)
   363→aws s3 ls s3://your-training-bucket || aws s3 mb s3://your-training-bucket --region us-east-1
   364→```
   365→
   366→#### Step 2 — Upload Tokenizer and Code to S3
   367→
   368→T3 coresets and T1 raw text are already on S3 — no upload needed for source data.
   369→Only the tokenizer and pipeline code need to be uploaded from your local machine.
   370→
   371→> **Note:** Alternatively, the tokenizer JSON files (`tokenizer.json`, `special_tokens_map.json`,
   372→> `tokenizer_config.json`) can be synced directly from the project GitHub repository onto the EC2
   373→> instance instead of uploading from local.
   374→
   375→```bash
   376→# Set your bucket name
   377→BUCKET="your-training-bucket"
   378→
   379→# Upload tokenizer (~5 MB)
   380→aws s3 sync tsai_131k_tokenizer/ s3://${BUCKET}/tsai_131k_tokenizer/ \
   381→  --region us-east-1
   382→
   383→# Upload code
   384→aws s3 sync . s3://${BUCKET}/tokenizer-code/ \
   385→  --region us-east-1 \
   386→  --exclude ".git/*" \
   387→  --exclude "*.pyc" \
   388→  --exclude "__pycache__/*" \
   389→  --exclude ".DS_Store" \
   390→  --exclude "datasets/*" \
   391→  --exclude "coresets/*" \
   392→  --exclude "tsai_131k_tokenizer/*"
   393→```
   394→
   395→#### Step 3 — Create IAM Role for EC2 (one-time setup)
   396→
   397→See [Section 4.3](#43-s3-setup-and-iam) for the full IAM policy. Quick setup:
   398→
   399→```bash
   400→# Save the trust policy
   401→cat > /tmp/ec2-trust.json << 'EOF'
   402→{
   403→  "Version": "2012-10-17",
   404→  "Statement": [{
   405→    "Effect": "Allow",
   406→    "Principal": {"Service": "ec2.amazonaws.com"},
   407→    "Action": "sts:AssumeRole"
   408→  }]
   409→}
   410→EOF
   411→
   412→# Create role and instance profile
   413→aws iam create-role \
   414→  --role-name TokenizationInstanceRole \
   415→  --assume-role-policy-document file:///tmp/ec2-trust.json
   416→
   417→aws iam create-instance-profile --instance-profile-name TokenizationRole
   418→aws iam add-role-to-instance-profile \
   419→  --instance-profile-name TokenizationRole \
   420→  --role-name TokenizationInstanceRole
   421→
   422→# Attach the inline policy (see Section 4.3 for full JSON)
   423→aws iam put-role-policy \
   424→  --role-name TokenizationInstanceRole \
   425→  --policy-name TokenizationPolicy \
   426→  --policy-document file:///tmp/tokenization-policy.json
   427→```
   428→
   429→#### Step 4 — Launch EC2 Spot Instance
   430→
   431→```bash
   432→BUCKET="your-training-bucket"
   433→RUN_ID="run_$(date +%Y%m%d)"
   434→KEY_NAME="your-key-pair"       # Replace with your EC2 key pair name
   435→SUBNET_ID="subnet-xxxxxxxx"   # Replace with a us-east-1 subnet
   436→SG_ID="sg-xxxxxxxx"           # Replace with your security group
   437→
   438→aws ec2 run-instances \
   439→  --region us-east-1 \
   440→  --image-id ami-0c02fb55956c7d316 \
   441→  --instance-type c5.9xlarge \
   442→  --key-name ${KEY_NAME} \
   443→  --security-group-ids ${SG_ID} \
   444→  --subnet-id ${SUBNET_ID} \
   445→  --iam-instance-profile Name=TokenizationRole \
   446→  --block-device-mappings '[{
   447→    "DeviceName": "/dev/xvda",
   448→    "Ebs": {
   449→      "VolumeSize": 200,
   450→      "VolumeType": "gp3",
   451→      "Iops": 3000,
   452→      "Throughput": 125,
   453→      "DeleteOnTermination": true
   454→    }
   455→  }]' \
   456→  --instance-market-options '{
   457→    "MarketType": "spot",
   458→    "SpotOptions": {
   459→      "SpotInstanceType": "one-time",
   460→      "InstanceInterruptionBehavior": "terminate"
   461→    }
   462→  }' \
   463→  --tag-specifications \
   464→    'ResourceType=instance,Tags=[{Key=Name,Value=tokenization-run},{Key=RunId,Value='${RUN_ID}'}]' \
   465→  --query 'Instances[0].InstanceId' \
   466→  --output text
   467→# Save the instance ID printed above
   468→```
   469→
   470→> **Tip:** If `c5.9xlarge` spot capacity is unavailable, try `c5.18xlarge` or `c5.4xlarge` as alternatives.
   471→
   472→#### Step 5 — SSH and Run Tokenization
   473→
   474→```bash
   475→# Wait ~60 seconds for instance to boot, then SSH
   476→INSTANCE_IP=$(aws ec2 describe-instances \
   477→  --instance-ids i-xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx \
   478→  --query 'Reservations[0].Instances[0].PublicIpAddress' --output text)
   479→
   480→ssh -i ${KEY_NAME}.pem ec2-user@${INSTANCE_IP}
   481→```
   482→
   483→On the instance, run the following bootstrap once:
   484→
   485→```bash
   486→# ---- BOOTSTRAP (run once after SSH) ----
   487→BUCKET="your-training-bucket"
   488→RUN_ID="run_$(date +%Y%m%d)"
   489→
   490→# Install Python deps
   491→sudo yum install -y python3.11 python3.11-pip tmux htop 2>/dev/null || \
   492→  sudo apt-get install -y python3 python3-pip tmux htop 2>/dev/null || true
   493→
   494→pip3 install numpy pandas pyarrow transformers datasets boto3 botocore tokenizers
   495→
   496→# Download code + data
   497→aws s3 sync s3://${BUCKET}/tokenizer-code/ ~/tokenizer/ --region us-east-1
   498→aws s3 sync s3://${BUCKET}/coresets/1B/ ~/tokenizer/coresets/1B/ --region us-east-1
   499→aws s3 sync s3://${BUCKET}/tsai_131k_tokenizer/ ~/tokenizer/tsai_131k_tokenizer/ --region us-east-1
   500→
   501→cd ~/tokenizer
   502→```
   503→
   504→Then run the tokenizer inside tmux (protects against SSH disconnects):
   505→
   506→```bash
   507→# Start a tmux session so the job survives SSH disconnect
   508→tmux new -s tokenize
   509→
   510→# Full production run — adjust BUCKET and RUN_ID
   511→BUCKET="your-training-bucket"
   512→RUN_ID="run_$(date +%Y%m%d)"
   513→
   514→python tokenize_curriculum.py \
   515→  --coreset-uri   s3://t2-datacurriculum-353/coreset_outputs/coresets/1B \
   516→  --dst-uri       s3://${BUCKET}/tokenized/${RUN_ID} \
   517→  --tokenizer-path ./tsai_131k_tokenizer \
   518→  --t1-base-uri   s3://t1-dataacquisition-datasets/processed_dataset/normalized_data \
   519→  --block-size    4096 \
   520→  --shard-size-mb 512 \
   521→  --num-proc      3 \
   522→  --file-parallelism 12 \
   523→  --drop-remainder \
   524→  --stage         1 \
   525→  --tokenizer-version v1 \
   526→  --tmp-dir       /tmp/tok_tmp \
   527→  2>&1 | tee ~/tokenize_$(date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S).log
   528→
   529→# Detach from tmux: Ctrl+B, then D
   530→# Reattach later: tmux attach -t tokenize
   531→```
   532→
   533→#### Step 6 — Monitor Progress
   534→
   535→```bash
   536→# Watch log (from another terminal)
   537→tail -f ~/tokenize_*.log
   538→
   539→# Check how many shards have been uploaded
   540→aws s3 ls s3://${BUCKET}/tokenized/${RUN_ID}/ --recursive | grep metadata.json | wc -l
   541→
   542→# Check progress state
   543→aws s3 cp s3://${BUCKET}/tokenized/${RUN_ID}/progress_state.json - | python3 -c \
   544→  "import json,sys; s=json.load(sys.stdin); print(f'Completed: {len(s[\"completed\"])} files')"
   545→
   546→# Monitor CPU usage on instance
   547→htop
   548→```
   549→
   550→#### Step 7 — Validate Output
   551→
   552→After the run completes (or any time):
   553→
   554→```bash
   555→# Run on instance or locally after syncing outputs
   556→python validate_shards.py \
   557→  --shards-dir /tmp/tok_out_synced \
   558→  --tokenizer-path ./tsai_131k_tokenizer \
   559→  --verbose \
   560→  2>&1 | tee ~/validation_$(date +%Y%m%d).log
   561→
   562→# Or sync metadata only for a quick check
   563→aws s3 sync s3://${BUCKET}/tokenized/${RUN_ID}/ /tmp/tok_out_synced/ \
   564→  --exclude "*.bin" --include "*/metadata.json"
   565→python validate_shards.py \
   566→  --shards-dir /tmp/tok_out_synced \
   567→  --tokenizer-path ./tsai_131k_tokenizer
   568→```
   569→
   570→#### Step 8 — Terminate Instance
   571→
   572→```bash
   573→# From your laptop — only after validating output
   574→aws ec2 terminate-instances \
   575→  --instance-ids i-xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx \
   576→  --region us-east-1
   577→```
   578→
   579→---
   580→
   581→### 4.2 Cost and Duration Breakdown
   582→
   583→#### Throughput Assumptions
   584→
   585→- TSAI 131K BPE tokenization throughput: ~3–5 million tokens/minute per vCPU
   586→- S3 same-region read latency (EC2 → S3, us-east-1): negligible for this workload
   587→- Each source parquet: ~50–200 MB compressed; ~5,000 unique source files total
   588→- With 12-way file-level parallelism, 12 source files download concurrently
   589→- S3 sustained read throughput to one instance: 2–5 GB/s for concurrent requests
   590→
   591→**Estimated wall time (20B tokens, c5.9xlarge, 12 workers × 3 HF procs = 36 cores):**
   592→- Tokenization: 20B tokens / (4M tokens/min × 36 cores) ≈ 140 min ≈ **~3–5 hours**
   593→- S3 download overhead: ~500 GB source data / 2 GB/s = ~4 minutes (negligible)
   594→- **Total: ~4 hours on c5.9xlarge**
   595→
   596→#### Instance Options
   597→
   598→| Instance | vCPU | RAM | `--file-parallelism` | `--num-proc` | Est. Duration | On-Demand $/hr | Spot $/hr (est.) |
   599→|----------|------|-----|---------------------|-------------|--------------|----------------|-----------------|
   600→| c5.xlarge | 4 | 8 GB | 1 | 4 | ~40 hrs | $0.17 | $0.05–0.07 |
   601→| c5.4xlarge | 16 | 32 GB | 8 | 2 | ~10 hrs | $0.68 | $0.15–0.22 |
   602→| **c5.9xlarge** | **36** | **72 GB** | **12** | **3** | **~4 hrs** | **$1.53** | **$0.35–0.55** |
   603→| c5.18xlarge | 72 | 144 GB | 20 | 3 | ~2.5 hrs | $3.06 | $0.65–1.00 |
   604→| r5.4xlarge | 16 | 128 GB | 8 | 2 | ~10 hrs | $1.01 | $0.22–0.35 |
   605→
   606→> Use `r5.4xlarge` only if large source parquets (>300 MB) cause memory pressure on `c5`.
   607→
   608→#### Total Cost Estimates (20B tokens, us-east-1)
   609→
   610→| Component | Cost |
   611→|-----------|------|
   612→| c5.9xlarge Spot (~4 hrs) | $1.40–$2.20 |
   613→| EBS gp3 200 GB (4 hrs) | $0.09 |
   614→| S3 data transfer (same-region) | **$0.00** |
   615→| S3 PUT requests (~500) | $0.003 |
   616→| S3 GET requests (~5,000) | $0.002 |
   617→| **Total compute** | **~$1.50–$2.30** |
   618→
   619→#### Output Storage Cost
   620→
   621→- Output shards: 20B tokens × 4 bytes = 80 GB minimum; with index files and metadata: ~90–100 GB
   622→- S3 Standard: $0.023/GB/month → ~**$2.07–$2.30/month**
   623→- S3 Infrequent Access (for archival): $0.0125/GB/month → **~$1.13–$1.25/month**
   624→
   625→#### Cost Comparison Summary
   626→
   627→| Strategy | Duration | Cost |
   628→|----------|----------|------|
   629→| c5.xlarge Spot (cheap, slow) | ~40 hrs | ~$2.00–$2.80 |
   630→| c5.4xlarge Spot | ~10 hrs | ~$1.50–$2.20 |
   631→| **c5.9xlarge Spot (recommended)** | **~4 hrs** | **~$1.50–$2.30** |
   632→| c5.18xlarge Spot | ~2.5 hrs | ~$1.65–$2.50 |
   633→| On-Demand c5.9xlarge (no Spot) | ~4 hrs | ~$6.12 |
   634→
   635→**The c5.9xlarge Spot is the sweet spot**: fastest per-dollar, fits comfortably in 72 GB RAM
   636→(12 workers × ~900 MB peak each = ~10.8 GB, leaving 61 GB headroom), and has high Spot
   637→availability in us-east-1 during off-peak hours (Spot interruption rate < 5% for 4-hour jobs).
   638→
   639→---
   640→
   641→### 4.3 S3 Setup and IAM
   642→
   643→#### IAM Policy for EC2 Instance
   644→
   645→Save as `tokenization-policy.json`:
   646→
   647→```json
   648→{
   649→  "Version": "2012-10-17",
   650→  "Statement": [
   651→    {
   652→      "Sid": "ReadCoresetIndex",
   653→      "Effect": "Allow",
   654→      "Action": [
   655→        "s3:GetObject",
   656→        "s3:HeadObject",
   657→        "s3:ListBucket"
   658→      ],
   659→      "Resource": [
   660→        "arn:aws:s3:::t2-datacurriculum-353",
   661→        "arn:aws:s3:::t2-datacurriculum-353/coreset_outputs/*"
   662→      ]
   663→    },
   664→    {
   665→      "Sid": "ReadRawTextData",
   666→      "Effect": "Allow",
   667→      "Action": [
   668→        "s3:GetObject",
   669→        "s3:HeadObject",
   670→        "s3:ListBucket"
   671→      ],
   672→      "Resource": [
   673→        "arn:aws:s3:::t1-dataacquisition-datasets",
   674→        "arn:aws:s3:::t1-dataacquisition-datasets/processed_dataset/normalized_data/*"
   675→      ]
   676→    },
   677→    {
   678→      "Sid": "ReadWriteTrainingBucket",
   679→      "Effect": "Allow",
   680→      "Action": [
   681→        "s3:GetObject",
   682→        "s3:PutObject",
   683→        "s3:HeadObject",
   684→        "s3:ListBucket",
   685→        "s3:DeleteObject"
   686→      ],
   687→      "Resource": [
   688→        "arn:aws:s3:::your-training-bucket",
   689→        "arn:aws:s3:::your-training-bucket/*"
   690→      ]
   691→    }
   692→  ]
   693→}
   694→```
   695→
   696→#### Destination Bucket Setup
   697→
   698→```bash
   699→BUCKET="your-training-bucket"
   700→REGION="us-east-1"
   701→
   702→# Create bucket
   703→aws s3api create-bucket \
   704→  --bucket ${BUCKET} \
   705→  --region ${REGION}
   706→
   707→# Block all public access
   708→aws s3api put-public-access-block \
   709→  --bucket ${BUCKET} \
   710→  --public-access-block-configuration \
   711→    "BlockPublicAcls=true,IgnorePublicAcls=true,BlockPublicPolicy=true,RestrictPublicBuckets=true"
   712→
   713→# Enable server-side encryption (AES-256)
   714→aws s3api put-bucket-encryption \
   715→  --bucket ${BUCKET} \
   716→  --server-side-encryption-configuration '{
   717→    "Rules": [{"ApplyServerSideEncryptionByDefault": {"SSEAlgorithm": "AES256"}}]
   718→  }'
   719→```
   720→
   721→#### S3 Prefix Structure
   722→
   723→```
   724→s3://t2-datacurriculum-353/            (T3 coreset index — read-only source, already exists)
   725→  coreset_outputs/coresets/1B/
   726→    selected_indices_part_shard000_batch000000.parquet
   727→    ...
   728→
   729→s3://t1-dataacquisition-datasets/     (T1 raw text — read-only source, already exists)
   730→  processed_dataset/normalized_data/
   731→    source=C4/
   732→      part-00759-8299c866-....parquet
   733→      ...
   734→
   735→s3://your-training-bucket/            (training output bucket)
   736→  tsai_131k_tokenizer/
   737→    tokenizer.json
   738→    special_tokens_map.json
   739→    tokenizer_config.json
   740→  tokenizer-code/
   741→    tokenize_curriculum.py
   742→    validate_shards.py
   743→    scripts/
   744→  tokenized/
   745→    run_20260303/                    ← one directory per run (timestamped)
   746→      progress_state.json            ← interrupt/resume state (updated after each batch file)
   747→      manifest.json                  ← global summary (written on successful completion)
   748→      selected_indices_part_shard000_batch000000/
   749→        shard_000/
   750→          tokens.bin                 ← 512 MB uint32 token IDs
   751→          tokens.idx                 ← spdl-compatible offset index
   752→          metadata.json              ← rich metadata (see below)
   753→        shard_001/ ...
   754→      selected_indices_part_shard000_batch000001/
   755→        ...
   756→```
   757→
   758→#### metadata.json Schema (per shard)
   759→
   760→```json
   761→{
   762→  "format": "megatron_bin_idx",

   790→
   791→---
   792→
   793→### 4.4 Spot Instance Interrupt Handling
   794→
   795→#### How AWS Spot Interrupts Work
   796→
   797→1. AWS decides to reclaim the instance (capacity needed elsewhere)
   798→2. **2-minute warning**: posted to EC2 Instance Metadata Service (IMDS)
   799→   - `GET http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/spot/termination-time`
   800→   - Returns HTTP 200 + timestamp when termination is scheduled; HTTP 404 otherwise
   801→3. **OS signal**: SIGTERM sent ~30 seconds before hard shutdown
   802→4. **Hard shutdown**: instance is terminated
   803→
   804→#### What `tokenize_curriculum.py` Does on Interruption
   805→
   806→The updated script handles this at three layers:
   807→
   808→**Layer 1 — IMDS polling daemon thread**
   809→- Background thread polls IMDS every 5 seconds
   810→- On HTTP 200 response: sets `_TERMINATION_DETECTED` threading.Event
   811→- Processing loop checks this flag before each source file download
   812→
   813→**Layer 2 — SIGTERM signal handler**
   814→- `signal.signal(SIGTERM, handler)` registered at startup
   815→- Also captures SIGINT (Ctrl+C) for local testing
   816→- Handler sets the same `_TERMINATION_DETECTED` event
   817→
   818→**Layer 3 — Graceful shutdown logic**
   819→- On termination detected: the processing loop breaks before the next source file
   820→- Partial `accumulated_blocks` in `ShardWriter` are **discarded** (not flushed)
   821→- Only **fully uploaded shards** (with `metadata.json` on S3) are counted as complete
   822→- `progress_state.json` on S3 records which batch files completed fully
   823→
   824→#### Checkpoint and Resume Mechanism
   825→
   826→The script has two levels of checkpointing:
   827→
   828→| Level | Granularity | How it works |
   829→|-------|-------------|-------------|
   830→| **Shard level** | Per 512 MB shard | `flush_shard()` checks if `metadata.json` exists at S3 key; if yes, skips |
   831→| **Batch file level** | Per coreset batch file | `progress_state.json` lists completed batch file URIs |
   832→
   833→On resume (instance relaunched with same arguments):
   834→1. `progress_state.json` is read from S3
   835→2. Already-completed batch files are skipped immediately (no S3 HEAD requests)
   836→3. In-progress batch files from the previous run are re-processed with shard-level skipping
   837→4. Fully fresh batch files are processed normally
   838→
   839→#### Auto-Restart with Auto Scaling Group (Optional)
   840→
   841→For fully automated restart without manual intervention:
   842→
   843→```bash
   844→# 1. Create launch template
   845→aws ec2 create-launch-template \
   846→  --launch-template-name tokenization-lt \
   847→  --launch-template-data '{
   848→    "InstanceType": "c5.9xlarge",
   849→    "ImageId": "ami-0c02fb55956c7d316",
   850→    "IamInstanceProfile": {"Name": "TokenizationRole"},
   851→    "KeyName": "your-key-pair",
   852→    "BlockDeviceMappings": [{
   853→      "DeviceName": "/dev/xvda",
   854→      "Ebs": {"VolumeSize": 200, "VolumeType": "gp3", "Iops": 3000}
   855→    }],
   856→    "UserData": "'$(base64 -w 0 userdata_auto_restart.sh)'"
   857→  }'
   858→
   859→# 2. Create ASG with Spot + capacity-optimized strategy
   860→aws autoscaling create-auto-scaling-group \
   861→  --auto-scaling-group-name tokenization-asg \
   862→  --launch-template LaunchTemplateName=tokenization-lt,Version='$Latest' \
   863→  --min-size 1 --max-size 1 --desired-capacity 1 \
   864→  --vpc-zone-identifier "subnet-xxxxxxxx" \
   865→  --mixed-instances-policy '{
   866→    "InstancesDistribution": {
   867→      "OnDemandBaseCapacity": 0,
   868→      "OnDemandPercentageAboveBaseCapacity": 0,
   869→      "SpotAllocationStrategy": "capacity-optimized"
   870→    },
   871→    "LaunchTemplate": {
   872→      "LaunchTemplateSpecification": {
   873→        "LaunchTemplateName": "tokenization-lt",
   874→        "Version": "$Latest"
   875→      },
   876→      "Overrides": [
   877→        {"InstanceType": "c5.9xlarge"},
   878→        {"InstanceType": "c5.18xlarge"},
   879→        {"InstanceType": "c5.4xlarge"}
   880→      ]
   881→    }
   882→  }'
   883→
   884→# 3. When the job is fully done, scale down to 0
   885→aws autoscaling set-desired-capacity \
   886→  --auto-scaling-group-name tokenization-asg \
   887→  --desired-capacity 0
   888→```
   889→
   890→**`userdata_auto_restart.sh`** — the script that runs on every instance launch:
   891→
   892→```bash
   893→#!/bin/bash
   894→set -e
   895→BUCKET="your-training-bucket"
   896→RUN_ID="run_20260303"   # Fixed: always resume the same run
   897→
   898→echo "[$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)] Bootstrap starting..."
   899→
   900→# Install deps (idempotent)
   901→yum install -y python3.11 python3.11-pip tmux 2>/dev/null || \
   902→  apt-get install -y python3 python3-pip tmux 2>/dev/null || true
   903→
   904→pip3 install numpy pandas pyarrow transformers datasets boto3 botocore tokenizers
   905→
   906→# Sync code and tokenizer (T3 and T1 data are already on S3 — no sync needed)
   907→aws s3 sync s3://${BUCKET}/tokenizer-code/ /home/ec2-user/tokenizer/ --region us-east-1
   908→aws s3 sync s3://${BUCKET}/tsai_131k_tokenizer/ /home/ec2-user/tokenizer/tsai_131k_tokenizer/ --region us-east-1
   909→# Alternative: clone tokenizer files from GitHub instead of S3 if preferred
   910→
   911→cd /home/ec2-user/tokenizer
   912→
   913→echo "[$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)] Starting tokenization (will auto-resume via progress_state.json)..."
   914→
   915→python tokenize_curriculum.py \
   916→  --coreset-uri   s3://t2-datacurriculum-353/coreset_outputs/coresets/1B \
   917→  --dst-uri       s3://${BUCKET}/tokenized/${RUN_ID} \
   918→  --tokenizer-path ./tsai_131k_tokenizer \
   919→  --t1-base-uri   s3://t1-dataacquisition-datasets/processed_dataset/normalized_data \
   920→  --block-size    4096 \
   921→  --shard-size-mb 512 \
   922→  --num-proc      3 \
   923→  --file-parallelism 12 \
   924→  --drop-remainder \
   925→  --stage         1 \
   926→  --tokenizer-version v1 \
   927→  --tmp-dir       /tmp/tok_tmp \
   928→  2>&1 | tee /home/ec2-user/tokenize_$(date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S).log
   929→
   930→EXIT_CODE=$?
   931→
   932→if [ ${EXIT_CODE} -eq 0 ]; then
   933→  echo "[$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)] Tokenization COMPLETE. Self-terminating instance."
   934→  # Upload logs to S3
   935→  aws s3 cp /home/ec2-user/tokenize_*.log s3://${BUCKET}/tokenized/${RUN_ID}/logs/ --region us-east-1
   936→  # Self-terminate to stop billing
   937→  INSTANCE_ID=$(curl -s http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/instance-id)
   938→  aws ec2 terminate-instances --instance-ids ${INSTANCE_ID} --region us-east-1
   939→else
   940→  echo "[$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)] Tokenization ended with exit code ${EXIT_CODE}. Instance kept for investigation."
   941→  # Do NOT self-terminate on failure — allows SSH investigation
   942→fi
   943→```
   944→
   945→> **Important:** The script does NOT self-terminate on failure (non-zero exit). This allows you to
   946→> SSH in and investigate. Failed instances will continue billing until you terminate them manually.
   947→
   948→#### Manual Resume After Spot Interruption
   949→
   950→If using a single instance (no ASG) and the instance was interrupted:
   951→
   952→```bash
   953→# 1. Launch a new instance (same configuration as Step 4)
   954→# 2. SSH in and run the exact same tokenize_curriculum.py command
   955→#    with the SAME --dst-uri arguments
   956→# 3. The script will automatically:
   957→#    a. Read progress_state.json from S3 → skip completed batch files
   958→#    b. For in-progress batch files: check shard metadata.json → skip complete shards
   959→#    c. Resume from the last checkpoint
   960→
   961→# Check what was completed before the interruption
   962→aws s3 cp s3://${BUCKET}/tokenized/${RUN_ID}/progress_state.json - | \
   963→  python3 -c "import json,sys; s=json.load(sys.stdin); \
   964→  print(f'Completed: {len(s[\"completed\"])} / total batch files')"
   965→```
   966→
   967→#### Estimated Cost Impact of Interruptions
   968→
   969→A Spot interruption at the midpoint of a 4-hour run on c5.9xlarge:
   970→- Work lost: at most 1 shard (512 MB) worth of tokens — the last partially-accumulated shard
   971→- Resume overhead: ~2–3 minutes to relaunch and re-download code/data from S3
   972→- Extra cost for re-run: one additional Spot-hour × $0.45 = ~$0.45
   973→
   974→For a 4-hour job at <5% interruption probability, expected extra cost is:
   975→- 5% × $0.45 = **~$0.02 expected extra cost per run**
   976→
   977→---
   978→

  1000→
  1001→```bash
  1002→python validate_shards.py \
  1003→  --shards-dir /tmp/synced_output \
  1004→  --tokenizer-path ./tsai_131k_tokenizer \
  1005→  --verbose
  1006→```

**TOOL CALL [Edit]:** {"replace_all": false, "file_path": "c:\\Raghu\\MyLearnings\\ERA_V4\\Capstone\\code\\p6\\P06_Tokenizer_Design_Lab\\experiments\\6_tokenizer_design_lab\\curriculum_tokenizer\\docs\\Tokenization_Pipeline_Review.md", "old_string": "---\n\n## 4. AWS Deployment Guide", "new_string": "### Known Platform Behavior: Local Filesystem Contention (Windows / NTFS)\n\nWhen running with `--file-parallelism > 1` against a **local (non-S3) destination**, all workers\nwrite their output shards to the same `<dst>/shards/` parent directory concurrently.  On Windows\nNTFS this causes filesystem-level serialization of directory-entry updates, resulting in\napproximately **2× slower throughput per batch** compared to sequential execution.\n\n**Observed numbers (small profile, 10 batches, `--file-parallelism 3`, Windows 11):**\n\n| Mode | Workers | Per-batch time | Total time |\n|------|---------|----------------|------------|\n| Sequential | 1 | ~55 s | ~550 s |\n| Parallel (direct write) | 3 | ~110 s | ~183 s |\n\nThe parallel run is still ~3× faster wall-clock than sequential despite the per-batch slowdown,\nbecause 3 batches run simultaneously.  However, per-core throughput drops by ~2×.\n\n**This does NOT affect S3 runs.**  S3 is an object store with no directory-lock semantics —\nconcurrent `PutObject` calls to keys under the same prefix are fully parallel and independent.\nThe observed contention is entirely a local-NTFS artifact.\n\n**The same behavior is expected on macOS HFS+/APFS** when multiple processes create\nsubdirectories under the same parent simultaneously (though APFS contention is generally lighter\nthan NTFS).\n\n**Recommendation:** For local testing, the 2× slowdown is acceptable — local runs use tiny\nprofiles and finish in minutes.  For production (S3, EC2), parallel throughput is unaffected.\nIf local parallel throughput becomes a bottleneck, each worker could be given an isolated\nper-batch staging directory; however, this adds a flatten step and is not worth implementing\nfor a test-only path.\n\n---\n\n## 4. AWS Deployment Guide"}
