The user provided the following reason for the rejection:  one Q before that. Is it an expected behavior that manifest.json &  progress_state.json not being written during the run is an expected behavior?

**ASSISTANT:** Yes, this is expected and by design:

**During a parallel run:**
- **`.done` markers** (`completed/<coreset_name>.done`) — written by each worker immediately after it finishes its batch. These exist mid-run.
- **`progress_state.json`** — written once *after the pool exits* (either clean completion or after `pool.terminate()` on interruption). Not written mid-run.
- **`manifest.json`** — written only on full clean completion. Never written mid-run or after interruption.

**Why this is safe for resume:**

On resume, the code reads `.done` markers *first* (not `progress_state.json`) to reconstruct the completed set. So even if `progress_state.json` doesn't exist yet (first run was hard-killed before pool.terminate() returned), the resume still knows which batches are done.

```
Parallel run flow:
  Worker 0 finishes batch → writes completed/batch000.done  ← visible mid-run
  Worker 1 finishes batch → writes completed/batch001.done  ← visible mid-run
  ...
  Pool exits (clean or SIGTERM) → writes progress_state.json
  All done → writes manifest.json
```

The `.done` markers are the durable per-worker checkpoint. `progress_state.json` is a convenience summary written at the end. `manifest.json` is the "job fully done" signal for the training team.

**ASSISTANT:** Good — the interrupt fired **correctly** (`[SIGNAL] Received signal 2`, `[INTERRUPT] Termination detected — stopping worker pool.`). Let me explain what we're seeing and what to expect on resume.

**Pre-resume state analysis:**

The SIGINT fired while workers were **mid-batch** (each batch had 5 source files; shards 001/002/003 were flushed after the 1st source file, but the workers hadn't finished all 5). So:
- 0 `.done` markers — correct, no batch fully completed
- 3 orphaned partial shards (`shard_001`, `002`, `003`) — these are valid data but represent partial work
- `progress_state.json` shows `Completed: 0/10`

**On resume:** all 10 batches will re-run. The atomic counter will start at `shard_004` (past the orphaned shards), so they won't be overwritten. The orphaned shards are harmless extra data.

**USER:** so i again stopped when 3 batches were completed.. but there were many incomplete shards there. Upon resume, does it not delete partial ones? Data should not be appended to the .bin file, it may cause duplicates

**ASSISTANT:** Great observation — this is a real data quality issue. Let me explain what's happening:

**No appending** — each shard is an independent directory (`shard_NNN/tokens.bin`). The resume never appends to existing `.bin` files. It writes fresh shard directories with new numbers (counter starts past existing ones).

**But there IS a duplicate problem:**
- The orphaned shards (e.g. shard_001, 002, 003 from the interrupted run) contain tokens from the **first T1 source file** of batches 0/1/2
- When those batches re-run fully, they re-process ALL 5 source files → their tokens appear again in the new shards
- Result: same documents appear in **both** the orphaned shards AND the new resume shards

**The fix**: The `.done` marker must record which shard names each completed batch owns. On resume, any shard NOT claimed by a `.done` marker is an orphan → delete it before re-running.

Let me implement this now:

**TOOL CALL [Read]:** {"file_path": "c:\\Raghu\\MyLearnings\\ERA_V4\\Capstone\\code\\p6\\P06_Tokenizer_Design_Lab\\experiments\\6_tokenizer_design_lab\\curriculum_tokenizer\\tokenize_curriculum.py", "offset": 240, "limit": 50}

   241→def load_progress_state(s3, dst_uri: str, tmp_dir: str) -> dict:
   242→    """Load progress_state.json from dst_uri. Returns empty state if not found."""
   243→    uri = f"{dst_uri.rstrip('/')}/progress_state.json"
   244→    state = download_json(s3, uri, tmp_dir)

   247→    return state

   319→def get_next_shard_idx_from_existing(s3, dst_uri: str) -> int:
   320→    """Scan <dst>/shards/ and return max existing shard number + 1.
   321→
   322→    Used to initialise the atomic counter on resume so parallel workers
   323→    never overwrite shards written by a previous run.
   324→    """
   325→    shards_dir = f"{dst_uri.rstrip('/')}/shards"
   326→    max_num = 0
   327→
   328→    if is_s3_uri(shards_dir):
   329→        if s3 is None:
   330→            return 1
   331→        bucket, prefix = parse_s3_url(shards_dir)
   332→        prefix = prefix.rstrip("/") + "/"
   333→        try:
   334→            paginator = s3.get_paginator("list_objects_v2")
   335→            for page in paginator.paginate(Bucket=bucket, Prefix=prefix, Delimiter="/"):
   336→                for cp in page.get("CommonPrefixes", []):
   337→                    name = cp["Prefix"].rstrip("/").split("/")[-1]
   338→                    if name.startswith("shard_"):
   339→                        try:
   340→                            max_num = max(max_num, int(name[6:]))
   341→                        except ValueError:
   342→                            pass
   343→        except Exception:
   344→            pass
   345→    else:
   346→        if os.path.isdir(shards_dir):
   347→            for name in os.listdir(shards_dir):
   348→                if name.startswith("shard_") and os.path.isdir(
   349→                    os.path.join(shards_dir, name)
   350→                ):
   351→                    try:
   352→                        max_num = max(max_num, int(name[6:]))
   353→                    except ValueError:
   354→                        pass
   355→    return max_num + 1

   491→        if not self.accumulated_blocks:
   492→            return None
   493→
   494→        # Parallel mode: acquire the shared counter to get a globally unique number.
   495→        # The lock is held only for the read-increment (microseconds), not during I/O.
   496→        if self._shard_counter is not None and self._shard_lock is not None:
   497→            with self._shard_lock:
   498→                shard_num = self._shard_counter.value
   499→                self._shard_counter.value += 1
   500→        else:
   501→            shard_num = self.shard_idx
   502→
   503→        shard_name = f"shard_{shard_num:03d}"
   504→        target_prefix = f"{self.dst_uri}/{shard_name}"
   505→        num_blocks = len(self.accumulated_blocks)
   506→
   507→        # Skip if shard already exists (resumability)
   508→        meta_key = f"{target_prefix}/metadata.json"
   509→        if key_exists(self.s3, meta_key):
   510→            print(f"    [SKIP] {shard_name} already exists")
   511→            self.shard_idx += 1
   512→            self.accumulated_blocks = []
   513→            self._reset_shard_counters()
   514→            return None
   515→
   516→        # Write .bin to temp
   517→        bin_path = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, "tokens.bin")
   518→        idx_path = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, "tokens.idx")
   519→        meta_path = os.path.join(self.tmp_dir, "metadata.json")
   520→
   521→        byte_offsets = []
   522→        current_byte_offset = 0
   523→
   524→        with open(bin_path, "wb") as f:
   525→            for block in self.accumulated_blocks:
   526→                byte_offsets.append(current_byte_offset)
   527→                arr = np.array(block, dtype=TOKEN_DTYPE)
   528→                raw_bytes = arr.tobytes()
   529→                f.write(raw_bytes)
   530→                current_byte_offset += len(raw_bytes)
   531→
   532→        # Final boundary
   533→        byte_offsets.append(current_byte_offset)
   534→
   535→        # Write spdl-compatible binary .idx
   536→        with open(idx_path, "wb") as f:
   537→            f.write(struct.pack(SPDL_IDX_HEADER_FMT, 1, BYTES_PER_TOKEN))
   538→            offset_arr = np.array(byte_offsets, dtype=np.uint64)
   539→            f.write(offset_arr.tobytes())
   540→
   541→        # Compute band/domain metadata
   542→        total_band_tokens = sum(self._band_counts.values()) or 1
   543→        band_distribution = {k: round(v / total_band_tokens, 4) for k, v in self._band_counts.items()}
   544→        dominant_band = max(self._band_counts, key=self._band_counts.get) if self._band_counts else ""
   545→
   546→        total_domain_tokens = sum(self._domain_counts.values()) or 1
   547→        domain_distribution = {k: round(v / total_domain_tokens, 4) for k, v in self._domain_counts.items()}
   548→        dominant_domain = max(self._domain_counts, key=self._domain_counts.get) if self._domain_counts else ""
   549→
   550→        # Write metadata
   551→        total_tokens = current_byte_offset // BYTES_PER_TOKEN
   552→        file_size = os.path.getsize(bin_path)
   553→        metadata = {

   555→            "idx_format": "spdl_v1",

**Why 39 shards exist but manifest shows only 25 (7 batches):**

There were actually **3 runs** of this pipeline:
- **Run 1**: Interrupted mid-batch (0 .done markers) → left orphan shards `shard_001-003` (partial work)  
- **Run 2**: You interrupted again after 3 batches completed → `shard_004-014` (3 full batches, ~11 shards) + 3 `.done` markers written
- **Run 3**: Resume completed remaining 7 batches → `shard_015-039` (25 shards), manifest written

The manifest's `total_shards: 25` only counts run 3's 7 batches. The 3 batches that completed in run 2 are missing from the manifest, even though their 11 shards are on disk. And shard_001-003 (partial/orphaned from run 1) are also on disk but garbage.

**Two separate bugs confirmed:**

| Problem | Root cause |
|---------|-----------|
| 39 shards on disk, manifest says 25 | Manifest only includes current-run's `all_stats`; pre-completed batches (from prior .done markers) are not included |
| shard_001-003 are orphan/partial duplicates | No orphan purge on resume |

**Token count confirms it:**
- Clean parallel run: `147,456` tokens (10 batches)
- Resume manifest: `102,400` tokens (only 7 batches — 3 pre-completed batches missing)
- 102,400 / 7 ≈ 14,629/batch; 147,456 / 10 = 14,746/batch ✓ consistent

**Three changes needed:**

1. Store `shard_names` + full stats in `.done` markers
2. On resume: **purge orphan shards** (delete any shard not claimed by a `.done` marker)
3. On resume: **load pre-completed stats from `.done` markers** → include in manifest

Shall I proceed with all three fixes?

---

## Analysis: Are the 3 fixes complete and correct?

### Fix 1 — Store `shard_names` in `.done` markers
**Covered:** worker writes `[shard_001, shard_003, ...]` alongside `total_tokens`, `num_shards`.

**Edge case — backward compatibility (critical):** Existing `.done` markers written by the old code have no `shard_names` key. If we load old markers and call purge, `claimed = set()` → ALL shards on disk get deleted, including valid ones. The fix: **skip purge if any marker lacks `shard_names`** — treat it as "can't safely determine orphans, do nothing."

---

### Fix 2 — Purge orphan shards on resume
**Covered:** before pool starts, delete any `shards/shard_N/` not in the union of claimed shard names across all `.done` markers.

**Edge case — first run, no `.done` markers:** `claimed = {}`. Purge logic would delete every shard on disk. **Fix:** only purge if at least one `.done` marker exists.

**Edge case — purge fails midway (power loss):** Some orphans deleted, some not. Next resume: purge runs again on the remainder. Idempotent. Safe.

**Edge case — `.done` marker write fails after all shards are already committed:** The batch has valid shards on disk but no marker. On resume: those shards look like orphans and get purged. The batch re-runs and re-creates the shards. **Acceptable** — the batch is re-processed once but the output is correct.

**Edge case — counter gap after purge:** After purging orphan `shard_001-003`, `get_next_shard_idx_from_existing` now sees the lowest remaining shard (e.g. `shard_004`). Counter starts at `max_remaining + 1`. New shards get `shard_015+`. There's a gap in numbering (`001-003` gone) but gaps are fine — the manifest references actual shard names, not a contiguous range.

**Edge case — sequential path:** Purge only belongs in the parallel path. Sequential path uses deterministic shard numbering and per-shard `key_exists` skip — it doesn't have orphan issues.

---

### Fix 3 — Include pre-completed stats in manifest
**Covered:** On resume, batches already done (`.done` markers exist) are skipped by the pool. Their stats never enter `all_stats`. The manifest would be incomplete.

**Fix:** After the pool exits, load all `.done` markers that were already completed before this run. Build stats entries from the markers and merge into `all_stats` (de-duplicated by `coreset_name` to avoid double-counting if a batch also appeared in the current run's pool results).

**Edge case — old markers lack full stats fields:** Old markers have `num_shards`, `total_tokens` but not `elapsed_seconds`, `num_docs`, etc. The manifest's per-file entries use `.get()` with defaults, so this is safe. The totals (`total_tokens`, `total_shards`, `total_tokens_dropped`) are what matter — `tokens_dropped` will be `0` for old markers (unknown), which slightly undercounts dropped tokens. Acceptable trade-off.

**Edge case — manifest reflects orphan shards' tokens even after purge:** If we purge the orphan shards from run 1 but some `.done` marker from run 2 claimed them as theirs... wait, no. Orphan shards by definition have no `.done` marker claiming them. Their tokens were never accounted for in any `.done` marker's `total_tokens`. So the manifest totals are always correct after purge.

---

### One additional edge case the fixes don't cover

**Mid-parallel-run `.done` marker + counter race:** Worker A finishes `batch_003` and writes its `.done` marker while Worker B is still mid-batch on `batch_007`. Then SIGTERM fires. Worker B gets killed mid-shard. On resume:
- `batch_003` → `.done` marker → claimed shards = `[shard_008, shard_009]`
- `batch_007` → no marker → shards `shard_010, shard_011` (partial from worker B) are orphans
- Purge deletes `shard_010, shard_011` ✓
- Counter starts at `9 + 1 = 10` ✓
- `batch_007` re-runs, gets shard `shard_010` onward ✓

This works correctly.

---

## Verdict

The 3 fixes are sound with two guards:
1. **Skip purge if any `.done` marker lacks `shard_names`** (backward compat)
2. **Skip purge if there are no `.done` markers** (first-run safety)

No other edge cases should cause data loss or duplication. Shall I proceed?

**ASSISTANT:** Now I have the full picture. Here are precise answers to both questions.

---

## Q1: Do more shards mean more token drops?

**No — shard count does NOT affect token drop rate.**

The `--drop-remainder` drop happens **once per batch**, not once per shard. Here's the exact mechanism (lines 897–928):

```
All T1 files processed → one shared buffer accumulates tokens
   ↓
Tokens chunked into 4096-token blocks → writer.add_block()
   ↓
When accumulated_blocks fills shard capacity → auto-flush shard (no data lost here)
   ↓
After ALL T1 files done: leftover buffer < 4096 tokens
   ↓  --drop-remainder
DROP this one partial tail block (at most 4095 tokens, avg ~2048)
```

The shard flush at line 546–547 only fires when a **full block** is ready. Partial blocks never go into a shard — they remain in `buffer`. Regardless of whether you have 1 shard or 100 shards, you lose **at most 1 partial block (< 4096 tokens)** per batch at the very end.

**What smaller `--shard-size-mb` does change:** more shards per batch, but same drops per batch.

**Math verification from the run:**
```
tok_out_resume:   total_tokens = 147,456 = 36 × 4,096  →  36 shards, 1 block each
tok_out_parallel: total_tokens should be same (same mock data, same block-size)
```
Drops would only differ if the token stream length itself changed — it doesn't.

---

## Q2: What exactly does `--file-parallelism N` parallelize?

The naming is slightly misleading. It does **not** parallelize T1 files within a batch. It parallelizes **entire coreset batches** (T3 rows):

```
--file-parallelism 3
        │
        ▼
Pool(processes=3)          ← line 1294
        │
        ├── Worker 0 → processes coreset batch_000000 end-to-end
        │     ├── downloads T1 file(s)
        │     ├── tokenizes (using num_proc=min(args.num_proc, 4) HF workers)
        │     └── writes shards to disk
        │
        ├── Worker 1 → processes coreset batch_000001 end-to-end  (parallel)
        │
        └── Worker 2 → processes coreset batch_000002 end-to-end  (parallel)
```

**`--num-proc` role** (line 854): inside each worker, `dataset.map(..., num_proc=min(args.num_proc, 4))` is the HuggingFace parallelism for **tokenizing rows within a single T1 file**. It spawns up to 4 sub-processes per worker to tokenize rows faster.

**Full concurrency picture with `--file-parallelism 3 --num-proc 2`:**
```
Main process
 ├── Worker A (batch 0)  → HF tokenizer with min(2,4)=2 sub-procs
 ├── Worker B (batch 1)  → HF tokenizer with min(2,4)=2 sub-procs  
 └── Worker C (batch 2)  → HF tokenizer with min(2,4)=2 sub-procs

Total OS processes = 1 main + 3 workers + (up to 6 HF sub-procs) = up to 10
```

**Why NTFS was slow:** all 3 workers write `shard_NNN/` subdirs under the same `shards/` parent simultaneously — Windows serializes that directory-entry update. On S3 there's no such contention.
